## 11.18.5.33 Combinatorial test design (V12-B brief: 3-way for law-critical capabilities)

### 11.18.5.33.1 Parameter model (call model, 8 parameters)

| # | Parameter | Values (count) | Value meanings |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Jurisdiction | `IN`, `EU-DE`, `EU-FR`, `UK`, `US-1P`, `US-AP`, `AE` (7) | `IN` India (TRAI TCCCPR, DLT, NCPR, DPDP); RP-IN-SMS, RP-IN-DPDP; `EU-DE` Germany (GDPR, ePrivacy Art. 13, UWG s.7, StGB s.201, TKG CLI rules); `EU-FR` France (GDPR, ePrivacy, Code de la consommation L.223-1 ff.; consumer opt-in from 11 Aug 2026, Bloctel discontinued (VR-69); Decree 2022-1313 hours); `UK` United Kingdom (UK GDPR, PECR regs 19/21, TPS, IPR 2018 recording); `US-1P` US one-party-consent state (TCPA, FCC 24-17 AI voice ruling, National DNC, 18 USC 2511(2)(d)); `US-AP` US all-party-consent state, e.g. California Penal Code s.632 (plus TCPA, DNC); `AE` UAE (PDPL Decree-Law 45/2021, Cabinet Resolution 56/2024 telemarketing, DNCR) |
| 2 | Direction | `INB`, `OUT` (2) | `INB` Inbound: caller dials a tenant number answered by the AI agent; `OUT` Outbound: platform places an AI call for a sales-engagement (promotional) purpose |
| 3 | Consent state | `NONE`, `CALLBACK_REQ`, `EXPRESS`, `PEWC`, `WITHDRAWN` (5) | `NONE` No lawful basis or calling consent on record; `CALLBACK_REQ` Documented callback request (web form or chat) whose notice said an AI assistant may call; `EXPRESS` Express consent to be called (verbal or form), not in the jurisdiction's written/registry-grade form; `PEWC` Prior express written consent (US E-SIGN grade) / DLT digital consent (IN) / documented opt-in (EU, UK, AE); `WITHDRAWN` Consent withdrawn or opt-out received on any channel |
| 4 | Language | `en`, `hi`, `ta`, `de`, `fr`, `ar` (6) | `en` English; `hi` Hindi; `ta` Tamil (Indic set representative); `de` German; `fr` French; `ar` Arabic |
| 5 | Recording mode | `OFF`, `NOTICE_ONLY`, `CONSENT_GIVEN`, `CONSENT_REFUSED` (4) | `OFF` Campaign or line configured not to record; `NOTICE_ONLY` Recording notice played; no affirmative consent captured; `CONSENT_GIVEN` Every party affirmatively consented (DTMF 1 or verbal yes captured and verified); `CONSENT_REFUSED` A party refused or did not answer the recording-consent prompt |
| 6 | DND / registry status | `NOT_REG`, `REG_FULL`, `REG_CATEGORY_OK`, `SCRUB_DOWN` (4) | `NOT_REG` Number not on the national do-not-call / preference registry; `REG_FULL` Number fully registered (NCPR full block, US National DNC, UK TPS, UAE DNCR; no French register since 11 Aug 2026); `REG_CATEGORY_OK` Registered with a category preference that allows this category (India NCPR partial); elsewhere treated as registered; `SCRUB_DOWN` Registry scrub service unavailable or scrub older than the allowed age |
| 7 | Time of day | `IN_WINDOW`, `BEFORE`, `AFTER`, `RESTRICTED_DAY` (4) | `IN_WINDOW` Weekday 11:00 recipient local time (inside every effective window); `BEFORE` 08:30 recipient local time; `AFTER` 21:30 recipient local time; `RESTRICTED_DAY` Sunday or public holiday, 11:00 recipient local time |
| 8 | Handoff trigger | `NONE`, `CALLER_REQ`, `LOW_CONF`, `SENSITIVE`, `DISCLOSURE_FAIL` (5) | `NONE` No trigger; AI completes the task; `CALLER_REQ` Caller asks for a person ('agent', 'human', 0 key); `LOW_CONF` Intent or answer confidence below the floor (0.70) twice in a row; `SENSITIVE` Price, discount, payment, contract, complaint, legal, clinical or emergency topic; `DISCLOSURE_FAIL` The AI disclosure audio failed to play or ASR did not confirm it within 2 s |

**Full space:** 7 × 2 × 5 × 6 × 4 × 4 × 4 × 5 = **134,400** combinations. **Value pairs:** 591. **Value triples (all 56 column triples):** 5,319. **Lower bound** for a 3-way array: the product of the three largest domains, 7 × 6 × 5 = 210 rows.

**Generation method.** A greedy AETG-style construction (`covering_array`, t = 3): each new row is seeded with an uncovered triple, the remaining parameters are filled value by value choosing the value that covers the most still-uncovered triples, 60 candidate rows are built per step and the best is kept. A verification pass (`verify`) asserts that every one of the 5,319 triples appears in at least one row; the script fails otherwise. Result: **273 rows** (1.30 × the lower bound), i.e. a 99.80% reduction of the full space while keeping every 3-way interaction.

**No constraints are removed from the space.** Combinations that are "impossible" in practice for one direction (for example a registry status on an inbound call) stay in the array; the oracle states that the parameter is **not evaluated** for that direction, which itself is a tested behaviour (the inbound path must ignore registry and window when answering, and must use staffing instead of the calling window for handoff).

**Oracle.** Expected results are computed by the function `oracle()` in the generator from the policy tables of file a (§11.18.5.3.1 consent and registry, §11.18.5.3.2 windows, §11.18.5.3.3 recording, §11.18.5.9 handoff, §11.18.5.11 language tiers, §11.18.5.17 caller ID). The same tables are loaded by AU-280/281/282 in the product, so a change to the law or to a pack is a change to one table, re-generated here and re-run in CI.

**Outcome mix of the 273 rows (regenerated v1.2 for the France opt-in and India Mon–Sat promotional window):** outbound ALLOW 16, BLOCK 72, HOLD 47; inbound ANSWER 138 (every inbound row also asserts the disclosure, recording and handoff outcome).

**Other models.**

| Family | Model | Method | Space | Rows | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-V12-Q-C3 | 8-parameter call model | 3-way covering array | 134,400 | 273 | US-22-ZQ01 (inbound rows), US-22-ZQ03 (outbound rows) |
| TC-V12-Q-RX | Jurisdiction (7) × direction (2) × recording mode (4) | **Exhaustive** (≤ 64) | 56 | 56 | US-22-ZQ05 |
| TC-V12-Q-VM | Jurisdiction (7) × AMD result (3: HUMAN, MACHINE, UNKNOWN) × consent (3: PEWC, EXPRESS, CALLBACK_REQ) | **Exhaustive** (≤ 64) | 63 | 63 | US-22-ZQ06 |
| TC-V12-Q-NX | Jurisdiction (7) × purpose (2: promotional, service) × number (3: compliant pool, wrong series, unregistered) | **Exhaustive** (≤ 64) | 42 | 42 | US-22-ZQ08 |
| TC-V12-Q-CP | Jurisdiction (7) × source (4: consented recording with coaching purpose, notice-only recording, staff notes, thread text) × role (4: learner, coach, HR, auditor) × output (3: learner feedback, team aggregate, HR export) × works agreement (2) | Pairwise (2-way; coaching is not a consequential external action) | 672 (153 pairs) | 31 | US-08-ZQ01 |
| | **Total generated cases** | | | **465** | |

## 11.18.6 Conflicts and proposed resolutions

All conflicts below are **resolved** by the Founder's adoption of V12 (DEC-782, DEC-779) or by this part's specification; none remains pending.

| Tag | V12 says | Baseline says | Resolution | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CONFLICT-779 (Part 11.2c) | SM-SVC-26 AI outbound calls and AI-conducted conversations; WF04 transcription | DEC-387 voice deferred; DEC-392 human-dialled only, no recording; Part 4.1 TR-R1 no SMS/voice outreach W0–W5 | DEC-782 adopts V12; this part specifies the controls; DEC-860/864 | Resolved (DEC-782) |
| CONFLICT-815 (Part 11.4d) | SVC-26 inbound AI answering, outbound AI calls, multilingual voice booking, permitted transcription | DEC-387, DEC-218, DEC-386, DEC-392, TR-R1 | As above; DEC-572 (pending) withdrawn; workflows Matched (§11.18.5.19) | Resolved (DEC-782) |
| CONFLICT-818 (Part 11.4d) | SVC-36-WF03 coaching from permitted meetings and conversations incl. voice | DEC-218, DEC-386 written notes only | Recordings allowed with coaching basis; DEC-386's employee-protection parts retained as V12 itself requires ("no automated employment decision"); DEC-870; DEC-575 (pending) withdrawn | Resolved (DEC-782, DEC-870) |
| [CONFLICT-1069] | SM-SVC-36 "review recordings and training outputs" | DEC-386: AG-045/AG-046 outputs private, team-level k ≥ 5, no employee evaluation | Keep learner-private outputs, k ≥ 5 aggregates and no HR use (compatible with V12 acceptance); only the recording exclusion is lifted | Resolved (DEC-870) |
| [CONFLICT-1070] | FSD ch13: SM-SVC-26-WF03 and WF04 have 0 AI-labelled steps | A live voice conversation and a call summary need model inference | V12 step classes kept; conversation turns run in session agents (AG-210/212) supervised by the deterministic steps; WF04-STEP-01 handler calls AG-213 as an AI sub-task registered under AU-150 class `ai_inference` | Resolved (this part; schema owner Part 11.4b informed) |
| [CONFLICT-1071] | WF01 "route or answer with evidence"; WF03 "ground answers" | Part 11.6b AG-100 guardrail "No free-form generation spoken to callers (FAQ text only)" | AG-100 stays the intent router; AG-210 speaks grounded generated answers in tier-A languages with sentence-level citation check and price-book exactness; tier-B languages remain verbatim-only (DEC-873) | Resolved (DEC-860, DEC-873) |
| [CONFLICT-1072] | WF02 "L2 mandatory approved effect; L3 only with separately approved bounded mandate where lawful" | Part 11.2c BRL-V12-B-07 / DEC-531 and Part 11.7b A170 "outbound calls L1 per campaign" | L1 per campaign digest then L2 per-contact dispatch; L3 bounded mandates for recurring service purposes under DEC-779 conditions | Resolved (DEC-864) |

**Inconsistencies observed in earlier parts (reported, not fixed).** (1) Part 11.4d CR-V12-D-4 sizes the voice adapters at 20–40 pd; the full voice scope here is 187–397 pd, so CR-V12-D-4's voice share is superseded (§11.18.5.32). (2) Part 11.4d proposes `eng_call` while V12 names the domain `voice_session`; V12 canonical name adopted. (3) App. B DTO v2 marks `recording_permission_ref` as required for all four SM-SVC-26 workflows, which would make unrecorded calls invalid; v3 makes it conditional [GAP-1078]. (4) Part 11.7b A170 lists pattern epic "EP-08" while Part 11.2a registers SM-SVC-26 under EP-22; this part uses EP-22. (5) Part 11.15a VR-13 (TCCCPR promotional time window) is not verified; the platform window 09:00–21:00 is applied as the stricter default.

## 11.18.7 Decisions

| DEC | Decision | Authority | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEC-860 | SM-SVC-26 (all four workflows) is **live-capable in Release 2** and **activates per jurisdiction** only when the country's voice pack is Approved by counsel, the activation checklist is complete and the SKU is active; connector framework and number inventory in Release 1 | Founder decision DEC-782 (28 Nov 2026), specified by this part | Supersedes the "Future (W6+), disabled" placement of Part 11.2c/11.4c; withdraws DEC-531 and DEC-572 (pending); TC-V12-D-126 retired |
| DEC-861 | Voice activation checklist (10 items, §11.18.5.3.4) is a blocking gate per tenant and country | Customer revision V12 (28 Nov 2026) with DEC-782 controls | Implemented by AU-170/AU-280; evidence in A170-Q7 |
| DEC-862 | AI self-identification standard D-1…D-7 with templates TPL-VO-DISC-01/02 in six languages; cannot be disabled | DEC-782 ("the AI identifies itself as AI at the start of the call") | AU-283; EU AI Act Art. 50(1); FCC 24-17; RP-IN-SMS-012 |
| DEC-863 | Recording requires the strictest party jurisdiction's basis; **platform default is affirmative consent of every party**; notice-and-object only where a pack approves it (IN, UK, US one-party) | DEC-782 ("all-party where required") | AU-282; §11.18.5.3.3 |
| DEC-864 | Outbound AI calls: L1 approval of the exact campaign digest by R02 and R10 (SoD), then L2 per-contact dispatch after AU-280; L3 bounded mandates only for recurring service purposes under DEC-779 conditions; on-call price, discount, terms, order and payment actions are never performed by voice AI | DEC-779, DEC-780, DEC-782 | Supersedes DEC-392 (human-dialled only); resolves CONFLICT-1072 |
| DEC-865 | Retention defaults: audio 90 days (max 12 months), transcripts 12 months (max 24), coaching items 12 months, consent evidence 6 years, CDR 24 months, suppression indefinite hashed; legal hold by R10 only | DEC-782 ("retention limits") | AU-286; packs may shorten |
| DEC-866 | Card data never enters platform audio or text: prevention, DTMF masking, payment pause, layered redaction, fail-closed persistence | DEC-782 ("redaction"); PCI DSS scope reduction (verify with QSA) | AU-285, AG-214 |
| DEC-867 | Provider-neutral voice stack with ports; cascaded ASR → LLM → TTS by default; S2S only if registry-approved with turn-level text events; no provider without DPA, no-training clause, region and eval pass | DEC-788 | ADR-250, ADR-251 |
| DEC-868 | Voice SLOs are release-blocking: turn latency p50 ≤ 1.0 s, p95 ≤ 1.6 s; barge-in ≤ 200 ms; disclosure ≤ 2.0 s; handoff p90 ≤ 60 s; availability per SLA tier | Customer revision V12 (28 Nov 2026) quality requirements; DEC-774 | AU-289; ADR-254 |
| DEC-869 | Synthetic voices: stock or licensed voices only; a custom brand voice needs the speaker's written consent, identity verification, contract clause and R10 + platform approval; provenance metadata on stored AI audio | DEC-782; DEC-788 (multimodal generation); RP-IN-ADV-017 | AG-218 checks; T-Q-03 |
| DEC-870 | Coaching from recordings (SVC-36-WF03) allowed only with a coaching basis for every party (and a works agreement where required); outputs learner-private, aggregates k ≥ 5, no HR/performance/pay use | DEC-782; V12 SVC-36 acceptance | Supersedes DEC-386 recording exclusion; keeps its employee protections; DEC-575 withdrawn |
| DEC-871 | Numbers: only inventory numbers; IN 140/1600 by purpose; STIR/SHAKEN A for US; no rotation to evade spam labels; 90-day cool-down; destination and transfer allowlists | CJ-IN-07; RP-IN-SMS-011; DEC-782 | AU-284 |
| DEC-872 | Voice pricing = platform per-minute fee + provider pass-through at cost + margin set by Finance; tenant meter counts started minutes per leg; monthly provider reconciliation ±1% | V12 BRD ch11 ("provider charges and managed service separately quoted") | AU-287; Part 11.20 cost model |
| DEC-873 | Language tiers: en, hi, de, fr tier A at launch; Indic set and Arabic tier B (verbatim scripts and booking only) until EVAL-Q thresholds pass | DEC-776 (locales); DEC-782 (multilingual voice booking) | AG-210/212 |
| DEC-874 | Voicemail rules VM-1…VM-6; no ringless voicemail anywhere | DEC-782 (consent per jurisdiction) | AU-280 |
| DEC-875 | Voice is never an authentication or approval factor; OTP before account data; approvals only in Admin with step-up MFA | Security review (voice-clone threat, T-Q-01/02) | AG-218 is a risk signal only |

### 11.18.7.1 Architecture decision records

| ADR | Title | Decision | Alternatives considered | Consequences |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-250 | Cascaded voice pipeline behind ports | ASR → model gateway → TTS with TelephonyPort/ASRPort/TTSPort/S2SPort contracts | All-in-one voice-agent SaaS; S2S-only | Deterministic guardrails possible on text; ~150–300 ms more latency than S2S, recovered by streaming and caching |
| ADR-251 | Media plane through a regional voice gateway | CPaaS media streams (WebSocket/TLS) terminate on the platform's gateway in the tenant's region; CPaaS recording disabled; SIP-trunk to self-hosted media server as the second option | CPaaS-hosted AI and recording; SIP-only from day 1 | Residency and recording control in platform; gateway is a new component to operate |
| ADR-252 | Recording storage | S3 per region, per-tenant KMS keys, Object Lock only for holds, hash chained into audit, redacted-only persistence | Database blobs; CPaaS storage | Crypto-shredding and DSAR feasible; KMS key count grows with tenants |
| ADR-253 | Consent ledger reuse | Voice consents stored in the existing scoped ledger (AU-172) with `voice_consent_evidence` for call-specific proof | Separate voice consent store | One withdrawal path across channels |
| ADR-254 | SLO measurement and degradation ladder | Per-turn spans; ladder L0…L4 controlled by AU-289 and the kill switch | Static failover only | Predictable behaviour under provider faults |

## 11.18.8 Expert persona review

| Persona | Review finding | Change made |
|---|---|---|
| Head of Conversational Voice AI | 1.6 s p95 turn latency is achievable only with streaming at every stage, prompt caching and short outputs; endpointing is the largest variable | Budget table with endpointing 250/400 ms; filler once on slow TTFT; ≤ 60-token replies; semantic turn detector |
| Telephony architect | CPaaS-side recording breaks residency and deletion guarantees; India promotional voice needs 140-series via the telemarketer chain, not a generic CPaaS DID | Recording in the regional gateway; CPaaS recording off; number pools by purpose with DLT binding (AU-284) |
| Telecom-regulation specialist | AI voice is "artificial voice" under TCPA, so PEWC for telemarketing; ePrivacy Art. 13 treats AI calls as automated calling; all-party recording laws make "notice only" unsafe as a default; France's calling hours are the narrowest in scope | Consent table, recording default all-party, FR window as effective window, verify flags and gaps GAP-1067/1068 |
| Contact-centre QA and coaching leader | Coaching without calibration drifts and erodes trust; managers will ask for league tables | κ ≥ 0.6 calibration, parity checks, learner-private by design, no HR scope; 2% human QA sample plus AG-217 on 100% of calls |
| AI governance lead | Voice-clone misuse cuts both ways (callers cloning customers; tenants cloning people); emotion recognition on individuals is high-risk in the EU | DEC-869/875, AG-218 as signal only, no individual sentiment, provenance marking, registry gates |
| FinOps Lead | Telephony and TTS dominate cost, not the LLM | Per-minute cost model; pass-through pricing (DEC-872); spend anomaly and caps |
